This is the "{}real scalar"{} type; if the {\itshape Scalar} type is already real numbers (e.\+g. int, float or double) then {\itshape Real\+Scalar} is just the same as {\itshape Scalar}. If {\itshape Scalar} is {\itshape std\+::complex$<$\+T$>$} then Real\+Scalar is {\itshape T}.

This function is mainly useful for blocks where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape block\+Rows} should equal {\itshape NRows} unless {\itshape NRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+: 
\begin{DoxyVerbInclude}
\end{DoxyVerbInclude}

\textbackslash{}newin\{3.\+4\}\+:

The number of rows {\itshape block\+Rows} and columns {\itshape block\+Cols} can also be specified at compile-\/time by passing \doxylink{namespace_eigen_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418}{Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$}, or Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$(n) as arguments. In the later case, {\ttfamily n} plays the role of a runtime fallback value in case {\ttfamily N} equals \doxylink{namespace_eigen_ad81fa7195215a0ce30017dfac309f0b2_ad81fa7195215a0ce30017dfac309f0b2}{Eigen\+::\+Dynamic}. Here is an example with a fixed number of rows {\ttfamily NRows} and dynamic number of columns {\ttfamily cols\+:} 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_tutorial___advanced_initialization___comma_temporary_8cpp_a5d2ded7a4c8830608d7b218f5f27c0a0_a5d2ded7a4c8830608d7b218f5f27c0a0}{mat}}.block(\mbox{\hyperlink{_bi_c_g_s_t_a_b__step__by__step_8cpp_acb559820d9ca11295b4500f179ef6392_acb559820d9ca11295b4500f179ef6392}{i}},\mbox{\hyperlink{tut__arithmetic__redux__minmax_8cpp_a39513f0f8d942bf9ef17b2e87ac6d34e_a39513f0f8d942bf9ef17b2e87ac6d34e}{j}},fix<NRows>,\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_aca7ce296424ef6e478ab0fb19547a7ee_aca7ce296424ef6e478ab0fb19547a7ee}{cols}})}

\end{DoxyCode}


This function thus fully covers the features offered by the following overloads \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_a3a98e606906dc31ef19e27a3b2ef2d12_a3a98e606906dc31ef19e27a3b2ef2d12}{block$<$\+NRows,\+NCols$>$(\+Index, Index)}, and \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_a350f08d56eb9ea6e62a55079bd9e9fa6_a350f08d56eb9ea6e62a55079bd9e9fa6}{block$<$\+NRows,\+NCols$>$(\+Index, Index, Index, Index)} that are thus obsolete. Indeed, this generic version avoids redundancy, it preserves the argument order, and prevents the need to rely on the template keyword in templated code.

but with less redundancy and more consistency as it does not modify the argument order and seamlessly enable hybrid fixed/dynamic sizes.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
Even in the case that the returned expression has dynamic size, in the case when it is applied to a fixed-\/size matrix, it inherits a fixed maximal size, which means that evaluating it does not cause a dynamic memory allocation. 
\end{DoxyNote}

\end{DoxySeeAlso}

This function is mainly useful for corners where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape c\+Rows} should equal {\itshape CRows} unless {\itshape CRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.

This function is mainly useful for corners where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape c\+Rows} should equal {\itshape CRows} unless {\itshape CRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+:

The number of coefficients {\itshape n} can also be specified at compile-\/time by passing \doxylink{namespace_eigen_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418}{Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$}, or Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$(n) as arguments. See \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_a9497c0f6429643b6088dfc5e06a2bcf7_a9497c0f6429643b6088dfc5e06a2bcf7}{block() } for the details.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
Even in the case that the returned expression has dynamic size, in the case when it is applied to a fixed-\/size vector, it inherits a fixed maximal size, which means that evaluating it does not cause a dynamic memory allocation.
\end{DoxyNote}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
an expression of the product of two sparse matrices. By default a conservative product preserving the symbolic non zeros is performed. The automatic pruning of the small values can be achieved by calling the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d}{pruned()} function in which case a totally different product algorithm is employed\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix}{C}}\ =\ (\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}*\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}}).\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d}{pruned}}();\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \textcolor{comment}{//\ suppress\ numerical\ zeros\ (exact)}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix}{C}}\ =\ (\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}*\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}}).\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d}{pruned}}(ref);}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix}{C}}\ =\ (\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}*\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}}).\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d}{pruned}}(ref,epsilon);}

This method is typically used in conjunction with the product of two sparse matrices to automatically prune the smallest values as follows\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix}{C}}\ =\ (\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}*\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}}).\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d}{pruned}}();\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \textcolor{comment}{//\ suppress\ numerical\ zeros\ (exact)}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix}{C}}\ =\ (\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}*\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}}).\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d}{pruned}}(ref);}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix}{C}}\ =\ (\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875_addc86e8508f14411ec98f521c520f875}{A}}*\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d_af60dc8214a22d8dfced07a33ec8e902d}{B}}).\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d_ac8d0414b56d9d620ce9a698c1b281e5d}{pruned}}(ref,epsilon);}

\end{DoxyCode}

The number of coefficients {\itshape n} can also be specified at compile-\/time by passing \doxylink{namespace_eigen_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418}{Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$}, or Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$(n) as arguments. See \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_a9497c0f6429643b6088dfc5e06a2bcf7_a9497c0f6429643b6088dfc5e06a2bcf7}{block() } for the details.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
Even in the case that the returned expression has dynamic size, in the case when it is applied to a fixed-\/size vector, it inherits a fixed maximal size, which means that evaluating it does not cause a dynamic memory allocation.
\end{DoxyNote}

The number of coefficients {\itshape n} can also be specified at compile-\/time by passing \doxylink{namespace_eigen_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418}{Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$}, or Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$(n) as arguments. See \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_sparse_matrix_base_a9497c0f6429643b6088dfc5e06a2bcf7_a9497c0f6429643b6088dfc5e06a2bcf7}{block() } for the details.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
Even in the case that the returned expression has dynamic size, in the case when it is applied to a fixed-\/size vector, it inherits a fixed maximal size, which means that evaluating it does not cause a dynamic memory allocation.
\end{DoxyNote}

This function is mainly useful for corners where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape c\+Rows} should equal {\itshape CRows} unless {\itshape CRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.

This function is mainly useful for corners where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape c\+Rows} should equal {\itshape CRows} unless {\itshape CRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.
